- **`SpriteRendererComponent.TiledSize` on `DrawMode=Sliced`/`Tiled` is NOT the world-unit draw-area size it appears to be from the `.d.mlua` description ("Sets the size of an area where the sprite will be drawn").** For this project's default placeholder sprite (RUID `1705e3c5b2c146ac9a699f96fb067408`, 24×24 native px, 100 PPU → 0.24 world-unit native size), empirically (confirmed via screenshot pixel measurement across three test values) the actual rendered size is `TiledSize × 0.24`, i.e. `TiledSize` behaves as a **multiple of the sprite's own native world-unit size**, not an absolute target size. A `TiledSize` of `(1.8, 0.6)` intended to mean "1.8×0.6 world units" instead rendered at ~0.43×0.14 — invisible/tiny until moved off the tile-map layer where it became noticeable as a small patch instead of a full chip. **Fix**: compute `TiledSize = desired_world_size / 0.24` (e.g. for a 1.72×0.5-unit chip, use `TiledSize = (7.17, 2.08)`). This affected `CharacterStandee.model`'s `CreateBg`/`CreateBorder`/root silhouette and `WorldButton.model`'s root fill/`Border` — all originally authored with the wrong (too-small) assumption; corrected 2026-07-16.
  **✅ Generalized and confirmed 2026-08-03 (5th session)**: the "specific to this one placeholder RUID?" question was open until this session. Placed two inline `MapBuilder.sprite()` test entities in `Lobby` side by side, both `DrawMode=1`(Sliced) `TiledSize=(2,2)`: the known placeholder (24×24px native) rendered a clean 40×40px box (confirmed against a self-measured screenshot scale — matches the established `×0.24` prediction), while a **different** RUID (`f92c6c9b127d4c3d8fedc54609a9464c`, a 176×124px "object"-category sign sprite found via `msw-search`) with the **identical** `TiledSize=(2,2)` rendered dramatically larger (~350+ px wide vs. the placeholder's 40px — an 8×+ difference, decisively distinguishing the two competing hypotheses). This confirms the formula generalizes: **`rendered_size = TiledSize × (native_width_px/100, native_height_px/100)` per axis, for any sprite** — the `×0.24` constant was never special to the engine, it was simply `24px/100` applying identically to both axes of that one square placeholder. **When authoring `TiledSize` for a new (non-placeholder) sprite, first look up its native `payload.width`/`payload.height` via `msw-search`'s `getResource`, then divide the desired world-unit size by `(width/100, height/100)` per axis** — do not assume `0.24` applies to a different sprite. Secondary finding from the same test: one `sprite`-typed RUID from an `"etc"`-category search result (a `52×24px` UI-style button texture reading "취소") rendered **nothing at all** in world `SpriteRendererComponent` under both `Sliced` and `Tiled` modes (silent failure, no build/runtime error) despite identical component wiring to the working reference — not every `type:"sprite"` search result is safe to use in a world `SpriteRendererComponent`; verify visually via a real screenshot before relying on a newly-found RUID, especially ones from `category:"etc"`.
- **Both `SpriteRendererComponent` (Sliced/Tiled) and `TextRendererComponent` anchor `TransformComponent.Position` at the visual box's BOTTOM-LEFT corner and grow UP+RIGHT as `TiledSize`/`RectSize` increases — not center-anchored.** Confirmed via precise pixel-bound measurement: two sprites given the *same* `Position` but different `TiledSize` (a fill + an intended "border" 0.1 unit larger on each axis) rendered with identical left edges and identical bottom edges, with the larger one sticking out **only** on the top and right — never the left or bottom. The initial `CreateBorder`/`WorldButton.Border` implementation (2026-07-16, first pass) set the border to the *same* position as its fill assuming center-anchoring, producing an "L-shaped" border visible only on two sides. **Fix**: derive every position from the desired *visual center*, not the naive shared-origin assumption: `position = desired_center - size/2` (for both sprite `TiledSize` and text `RectSize`, per axis). Re-verify with a pixel-bound measurement (not just eyeballing a screenshot) after any resize — a small enough size mismatch can look "close enough" at a glance while still being measurably lopsided.
- **`TextRendererComponent`'s effective vertical text position does not cleanly follow the `position = center - RectSize/2` formula in practice**, even though it fixed the horizontal case reasonably well. After applying the formula, measured text still sat low in its box (bottom edge flush with the border's bottom, ~30px of empty space above). Root cause not fully isolated (font line-height/ascender padding is the leading suspect) — resolved pragmatically by measuring the actual rendered glyph bounding box against the border's bounding box in the same screenshot and nudging `Position` empirically (not recomputing from a formula) until both centers matched. **When tuning text position precisely**: don't trust the `RectSize`-based formula alone for the Y axis — take a screenshot, measure both boxes' pixel bounds directly (same image, no cross-coordinate-system conversion needed), and nudge from the measured delta.
  **✅ Root cause re-investigated 2026-08-03 (5th session) — revises the "font padding" theory above.** Tested two things empirically in `Lobby` via inline `TextRendererComponent` entities + pixel-measured screenshots: (1) swapping `VerticalAlignment` from `Middle`(512, this project's default) to `Geometry`(4096, "aligns the center of the entire text to the center of the text box" — a mode built to exclude font line-height/ascender metrics) **made no meaningful difference** — both rendered the "Hg" test string at the same measured pixel position (356 vs 358.5, ~2px apart, within noise), ruling out "switch the alignment enum" as a fix. (2) **Growing `RectSize.y` from `1` to `3` (3× taller box, same `Position`) produced a byte-identical measured text position** (`x=[624,656] y=[344,368]` in both cases) — the text did not shift at all despite the box supposedly growing around it. **This means `RectSize.y` does not factor into where text vertically renders in this engine** — the glyph's visual center sits close to `Position.y` itself (measured ~0.12 world units above it, plausibly just "Hg"'s own cap-height/descender asymmetry, not a padding bug), independent of box size. **Practical implication — this changes the recommended formula**: for `TextRendererComponent`, do **not** apply the sprite-style `Position = desired_center - RectSize/2` on the Y axis — that formula subtracts half the box height that the engine never actually uses for centering, which is likely the real source of the "text sits low" symptom this project kept hitting, not font padding. **For vertical placement, set `Position.y` close to the desired visual center directly** (skip the `RectSize.y/2` term), then fine-tune with one screenshot-measured nudge same as before. The `X` axis is unaffected by this finding — the separate, still-unresolved `HorizontalAlignment=Center` quirk documented below ("glyph renders flush against the box's left edge") reproduced identically in this session's test too, so the existing screenshot-measure-and-nudge practice for X remains necessary.
- **When text content is wider than its container's actual rendered size** (easy to get wrong given the `TiledSize` scaling bug above), the overflow reads as messy/unaligned even with a technically-correct center. `CharacterStandee`'s `NameTag` at `FontSize 2.15` measured ~192px wide against a ~154px-wide chip — center-aligned but overflowing both edges (worse on one side than the other, making it look off-center too). Fixed by reducing `FontSize` (2.15 → 1.6) so the rendered text width comfortably fits inside the chip, in addition to the center-position correction. Check rendered text width against the container's actual (not nominal) size before trusting a "looks roughly centered" screenshot glance.
- **`maker_mouse_input`'s `position` does NOT reliably match `maker_screenshot`'s reported `width`/`height` despite the tool description.** Empirically (via temporary `log()` of `_UILogic:ScreenToWorldPosition(event.TouchPoint)` compared against known standee world positions), the working input↔world-X relationship for `choice_map` was `input_x = 286.5 + world_x × 150` (and the analogous `input_y = 185.25 + world_y × 150`) — a 150 px/world-unit scale, not the ~93 px/unit a naive `screenshot_width(1193) / 12.8_units` calculation would predict, and with an X-offset that doesn't correspond to a screen-centered camera either. Clicking at raw screenshot pixel coordinates (assumed to be the touch-input space) silently misses the intended target with no error. **When precise click targeting matters**: add a temporary diagnostic `log()` of `ScreenTouchEvent`'s resulting world position inside the handler, click at a rough guess, read the log, then solve the linear map from ≥2 real (input, world) pairs before trusting further clicks — don't assume the screenshot pixel grid is the click-input grid.
- **A child entity's entity-level `Enable` (not `Component.Enable`) can get "stuck" for one specific instance and stop responding to further client writes, with no error and no other symptom.** `CharacterStandee.model`'s `CreateBg` toggle (`createBg.Enable = (data == nil)`) worked correctly on 5 of 6 placed `Standee` instances but silently stayed `true` on `Standee3` specifically, even immediately after a fresh `RefreshStage()` call with confirmed-correct `data` — verified live via `maker_execute_script`: reading `createBg.Enable` (entity-level) stayed `true` no matter what, while `createBg.SpriteRendererComponent.Enable` (component-level) responded immediately and reliably to the same write. Visually this produced the reported "two overlapping buttons" — the gold `CreateBg` fill stuck visible on top of the always-on `CreateBorder`, on an otherwise-occupied slot that should show only the plain brown border. Root cause not fully isolated (a stale per-entity `@Sync` value surviving repeated `placeModel` destructive re-syncs is the leading theory, since `Standee3`/`Standee4` are the two instances with slightly different historical Y positions), but the **fix is to toggle the renderer's own `SpriteRendererComponent.Enable` instead of the entity-level `Enable`** wherever a specific instance's entity-level toggle might have gone stale. `Glow`'s entity-level `Enable` toggle (same code shape) was left alone since it has a long verified track record working correctly — don't "fix" a toggle that isn't showing symptoms, but if a *specific instance* silently stops responding to an `Enable` write with no error, suspect entity-level vs. component-level `Enable` before anything else, and confirm which one is actually stuck via a live `maker_execute_script` read — a static file inspection (`ModelBuilder`/`MapBuilder` read) will show identical data across "good" and "bad" instances since this is a runtime-only desync, not a saved-data bug.
  **↳ Re-tested 2026-08-03 (5th session) — no longer reproducible, root cause left unconfirmed.** Live in `choice_map` (client context, `maker_execute_script`), toggled `CreateBg.Enable` (entity-level, the exact field that used to stick) `true→false→true` on all 6 `Standee1..6` instances at once. **All 6 — including `Standee3` — read back correctly at every step** (`before=true afterSetFalse=false afterSetTrue=true`, no exceptions). The stuck state is gone. Static `.map` inspection also showed no anomaly (`revision`/`enable`/`origin` identical across all 6, as the original note predicted a file read would show). **Most likely explanation**: this looked like a genuine one-off client-side `@Sync` desync (per the original "stale value surviving a destructive `placeModel` re-sync" theory), and it has since been overwritten by one of the many full `placeModel()` re-syncs this project ran on these same 6 entities afterward (font pass, position patches, etc. — see the dated entries above) — each one rewrites the entity fresh, so any stale runtime state from *before* that point can't survive it. **Practical conclusion**: nothing further to fix — this was very likely a transient runtime artifact tied to a specific historical edit sequence, not a standing defect in `CharacterStandee.model` or in this project's `Enable`-toggle code. The `SpriteRendererComponent.Enable` workaround already shipped stays in place regardless (it's strictly safer than entity-level `Enable` and costs nothing), but don't casually revert to entity-level `Enable` without cause — the original trigger sequence is not fully understood and could theoretically recur after some future bulk `placeModel` operation.
- **A world-text label centered inside a button/box container must have its `Position`/`RectSize` derived from the container's actual visual center, not eyeballed against a screenshot.** `HorizontalAlignment=Center`/`VerticalAlignment=Middle` only centers the text *within its own `RectSize` box* — it does nothing to align that box with a sibling sprite's box. Since `TransformComponent.Position` is the box's bottom-left corner (see the `TiledSize`/`RectSize` anchor Gotcha above), the box's true center is `Position + RectSize/2`, and two boxes are visually concentric only when that computed center matches. `CharacterStandee.model`'s `NameTag` (the "캐릭터 생성" / "Lv.N" label) had drifted off the `CreateBg` gold chip's center by roughly (+0.14, +0.20) world units — a past session's empirically-eyeballed `Position` (0.20, -1.65) was close but not exact. **Fixed 2026-07-20** by computing `CreateBg`'s real center from its `TiledSize` (`TiledSize × 0.24` per the ratio above) → `(0.86, -1.55)`, then solving `NameTag.Position = center - NameTag.RectSize/2` = `(0.06, -1.85)` for `RectSize=(1.6, 0.6)`. Applied to both the `.model` template (so future `placeModel` re-placements inherit it) **and** patched directly onto all 6 already-placed `Standee1..6/NameTag` map instances via `MapBuilder.patchComponent` (editing the template alone does not retroactively update already-placed instances — `placeModel` only mirrors at placement time). **Established convention going forward**: any button or box-group label in this project should be center-aligned by default (matches the existing `WorldButton.model` `Label`, which was already correctly centered against its `Border`/root fill) — when adding a new labeled box, derive the label's `Position` from `container_center - label_RectSize/2` rather than tuning by eye, and only fall back to a small screenshot-measured nudge for the vertical axis if the font's line-height padding still reads visually low (see the `TextRendererComponent` vertical-position Gotcha above).
- **A world-entity's click hit-test region must be re-checked whenever its visual is repositioned.** `CharacterSelectStageController.OnScreenTouched`'s per-standee hit box was tuned for the old CreateBg/NameTag position (near the foothold); moving that chip further down (§ above) silently left it outside the old `dy` lower bound, so the visibly-repositioned button briefly stopped registering clicks entirely — caught only by an end-to-end click test after the visual change, not by build/lint (no error, box is just numeric). Any future reposition of a child entity referenced by a screen-space or world-space hit-test must re-derive the hit box's bounds, not just eyeball "does it look right."

- **This avatar item index has no separate "hair color" or "eye color" property — color is baked into each item RUID.** `CostumeManagerComponent`'s 17 `Custom*Equip` slots (see `msw-avatar`) include `CustomHairEquip` and `CustomFaceEquip`, but neither has a companion color/tint field, and there is no `Eye` category at all (eyes/expression are part of `Face`). Each differently-colored hairstyle, or differently-colored/expressioned face, is a **distinct `avataritem` RUID** — e.g. `searchAvatarItems('hair', { categoryFilter: ['hair'] })` with the default `canonicalOnly: true` dedupes same-style color variants down to one representative per group, which is why the original 6-item hardcoded pools read as "not much hair/eye variety" even though the underlying catalog has far more. **Fix (2026-07-20)**: re-queried with `canonicalOnly: false` and `topK: 60` for both `hair` and `face` categories, which returns many distinct RUIDs spanning a wide `payload.color_hex` range (confirmed e.g. hair colors from near-black `#323232` to purple `#903bea` to green `#99c133`), and replaced the 6-item `hairPool`/`facePool` in `CharacterSelectStageController.InitRandomPools()` with the full 60-item lists. If more variety is ever needed again, the same `canonicalOnly: false` + larger `topK` re-query is the correct lever — not a new color field, since one doesn't exist in this system.

- **`ModelBuilder`'s `typeKey` list (`bool`/`int`/`float`/`string`/`vector2`/`vector3`/`quaternion`/`collision_group`/`data_ref`/...) has no `color` entry — passing `'vector3'` for a `Color`-typed value (e.g. `SpriteRendererComponent.Color`, `TextRendererComponent.FontColor`) silently writes the wrong `ValueType` (`MOD.Core.MODVector3` with `r/g/b/a` fields jammed into an `x/y/z` schema) instead of `MOD.Core.MODColor`.** Confirmed by inspecting a freshly-written value via `ModelBuilder.getChild(...).Model.Values` — no runtime error, no `write()`/`validate()` complaint, just a mistyped entry that the engine would likely reject or misread at load. The builder's automatic type-preservation only helps when *patching an existing* Color value inherited from a template (it detects the existing entry's `ValueType` isn't `System.String` and preserves it) — it does **not** help when authoring a **brand-new** Color value on a child that has no prior entry for that `(TargetType, Name)` pair. **Fix**: pass the value pre-wrapped with its own `$type` (`{ $type: 'MOD.Core.MODColor, MOD.Core', r, g, b, a }`) **and** pass the fully-qualified assembly string as `typeKey` (`'MOD.Core.MODColor, MOD.Core, Version=26.5.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=null'`) — an unrecognized `typeKey` string is used verbatim for `ValueType.type` and `wrapValue` passes the object through unchanged when it doesn't match any of the builder's known shapes, so the manually-supplied `$type` sticks. Re-verified via `getChildValue`/`getChild(...).Model.Values[...].ValueType` showing the correct `MOD.Core.MODColor` type. **Whenever authoring a brand-new (not template-inherited) Color-typed value through `ModelBuilder`, use this explicit-`$type` pattern — never a bare `vector3`/`vector2` typeKey or an untyped plain `{r,g,b,a}` object.**

- **"The math says the box and text are centered on the same point" is not proof they render aligned — verify with actual pixel measurement, not the `position = center - size/2` formula alone, especially when a prior fix already required an empirical nudge.** When the user reported the "캐릭터 생성" button box still didn't line up with its text (2026-07-20, after a first fix pass had already shifted the box vertically based on formula reasoning), a from-scratch recompute showed `NameTag.Position + RectSize/2` and `CreateBg.Position + TiledSize×0.24/2` landing on the **exact same** world point — mathematically "already centered." The actual bug was invisible to that analysis: (1) vertically, the text glyph really does render measurably lower than its `RectSize` box's geometric center (the known font-baseline/ascender-padding quirk — confirmed again here via pixel measurement, glyph sat with the box's rendered gap almost entirely above the text), and (2) horizontally — the far bigger error — `HorizontalAlignment=Center` did **not** visually center the text within its `RectSize` box at all; the glyph cluster rendered flush against the box's left edge with the entire right ~2/3 of the box empty, roughly **0.5 world units off-center** (measured: text center at local pixel x≈156 vs. box gold-interior center at x≈214, a delta far too large to be a rendering-quirk nudge — this was a real, large misalignment the formula had no way to predict). **Method that actually found and fixed it**: `maker_screenshot` in play mode → load the PNG in PowerShell (`[System.Drawing.Bitmap]::FromFile(...)`, `GetPixel(x,y)`) → scan pixel rows/columns for color-based region boundaries (gold fill ≈ `(217,166,46)`, border ≈ `(74,46,23)`, text glyph ≈ very dark `(31,23,8)`-ish, teal water background has `G`/`B` ≫ `R`) → compute each region's bounding box and center directly from measured pixel extents, not from the authored `Position`/`RectSize` values. Converting pixel deltas to world units used the screenshot's own capture scale (`resolution.width / screenshot.width` × the previously-measured `150 px/world-unit` reference constant — for a `1193`-px-wide capture at `1920` reference resolution, effective scale ≈ `93 px/unit`). Applied the measured deltas as **plain position shifts to `CreateBg`/`CreateBorder`** (per the user's explicit instruction to move the box, not the text) via `MapBuilder.patchComponent`, re-screenshotted, and re-measured — took **two** correction passes (a coarse first shift, then a ~10px residual correction) before both the horizontal and vertical box-vs-text centers matched within ~1px. **Lesson for any future box/label alignment work in this project**: don't trust `HorizontalAlignment`/`VerticalAlignment` + `RectSize` math to predict rendered centering — screenshot-and-measure is the only reliable method already proven in this codebase, and a "the numbers already match" conclusion from formula alone should be treated as unverified, not as a fix.

- **The `msw-search` resource API (`searchAvatarItems` / `getResource` / any `avataritem` response) has no created-at / updated-at / release-date field anywhere in its schema.** When the user asked for "the most recently updated hair and eyes," there is no way to sort by true recency. The only proxy available is the `dname` field's numeric suffix (e.g. `"hair-12809"`, `"face-10188"`) — it *appears* to be an incrementing internal catalog id (spot-checked: items with a visibly newer/more elaborate style consistently carried higher numbers than older basic styles in this project's samples), so sorting a broad `topK` fetch by that number descending is a reasonable "probably newer" ordering — **but it is not a guaranteed release-date sort** and should be described to the user as an approximation, not a fact. Used this to add 20 more hair + 20 more face RUIDs (highest-`dname` items not already in the existing 60-item pools) on top of the 2026-07-20 pool-widening fix, bringing both pools to 80 items each.

- **`Lobby.map`'s old "foothold row near x=8" no longer exists — confirmed 2026-08-03 (5th session), correcting this entry's original claim.** The original note below (kept for history) assumed a foothold segment near x=8 existed and blamed a `layer`/`groupID` mismatch for a fall-through. Re-investigated via `MapBuilder.getFootholds()` grouped by `groupID`: **the current `Lobby.map` has exactly 2 foothold groups, both entirely within `x=54.05~89.95`** (group 1: town-square ground `y=-1.22` + a vertical boundary wall at each end down to `y=-8.70`; group 2: the upper walkway at `y=1.78`, `x=59.45~68.35`). **No foothold segment exists anywhere near `x=8`** — every real entity in the map (`ladder-102`, `npc-7`, `portal-3`, all 6 `SpawnLocation`s) also sits within `x=55~64`, confirming nothing currently depends on the old x=8 area. The terrain there was evidently fully removed (not just "vestigial") at some point after the town-square rebuild, and this doc's "World map inventory" row + the original Gotcha below were never updated to match. **Practical implication**: `Vector3(8, -0.85, 0)` is not a valid spawn/teleport target in the current `Lobby.map` at all — do not reuse that coordinate from old notes. **General lesson reaffirmed**: before placing anything that depends on a specific platform (NPC, portal, a precise spawn/teleport point), inspect `getFootholds()` grouped by `groupID`/`layer` fresh — a doc's foothold-position claims can go stale silently when terrain is edited later without the doc being revisited.
  <details><summary>Original entry (2026-07-23 or earlier, superseded — kept for history)</summary>

  `Lobby.map` has multiple foothold rows at different Y (span y=-6.9~-1.25) — spawning "0.4 above the first foothold segment found near x=0" does not guarantee landing on that segment. Teleporting a player to `Vector3(8, -0.85, 0)` (0.4 above the y=-1.25 row, which does span x=8 per `MapBuilder.getFootholds()`) still resulted in the player falling through to a much lower foothold (~y=-6.62, confirmed `onGround=true` via `maker_execute_script` — safe landing, not a void-fall bug). Root cause not yet isolated (candidates: the y=-1.25 row belongs to a foothold `layer`/`groupID` that isn't the one directly under the spawn X, or it's a non-colliding decorative walkway) — not investigated further since Task scope was only "PLAY enters Lobby without breaking," which is satisfied.

  </details>

- **A `@Component`'s state on one map's entity is unreachable from the client once the local player has moved to a different map — confirmed empirically, not just per docs.** `_EntityService:GetEntityByPath("/maps/choice_map")` returned `isvalid=false` from a `maker_execute_script` call made *after* the local player had already teleported to `Lobby`, even though the same entity/component was reachable and fully functional moments earlier while still in `choice_map`. This matches the documented "`Entity`/`Component` references are dropped on map transition" rule, but the practical implication is broader than "don't hold a stale reference" — the *entity itself* (and therefore every `_T` field on its components) becomes inaccessible, not just a previously-cached reference to it. **Any state a script needs to read/write from more than one map must live on a `@Logic` (world-session-scoped, no `self.Entity`), not on a map-scoped `@Component`** — this is exactly why `CharacterRosterLogic` exists (see Key files). `/ui/*` entities are NOT subject to this — they're a global overlay layer, not part of any specific game map, and stayed reachable (`CharacterCardGroup`/`SquadFormationGroup`) from both `choice_map` and `Lobby` throughout testing.
- **`PortalComponent.PortalEntityRef` (an `EntityRef` field) cannot be set through either builder — confirmed by grepping both `.cjs` sources for `EntityRef` (zero hits) after the `typeKey` list (`bool`/`int`/`vector2`/`vector3`/`collision_group`/`data_ref`/...) came up short.** The official Nexon docs describe setting a portal's destination as a manual Property Editor action (click the `[◉]` reference button next to `TargetPortal`, pick the destination from a picker window) — there's no documented raw-JSON shape to copy either. **This manual step was asked of the user twice and missed both times** (2026-07-23), which is what finally drove the fix below rather than a third attempt. **Resolution: abandoned the native `PortalComponent`/`PortalEntityRef` pairing for the Lobby↔choice_map return trip entirely.** `LobbyStageController.OnKeyDown` does its own `KeyboardKey.UpArrow` + box-distance check against `portal-3`'s own `BoxOffset`/`BoxSize` (still reused for the hit-test, so the portal's collision area stays visually/functionally meaningful), then `CharacterRosterLogic.RequestReturnToSelect()` does a direct `TeleportToMapPosition` — no `EntityRef` linking anywhere. The dummy `ReturnPortal` arrival entity in `choice_map` (created for the native approach) was deleted as dead weight. **Lesson: if a builder coverage-gap workaround requires a manual Maker UI step, don't assume the user will complete it — if the step gets missed once, prefer replacing the mechanism with something fully AI-editable over asking again.**
- **A map-scoped `@Component`'s `OnBeginPlay` DOES refire when the local player re-enters that map later in the same Play session — confirmed empirically, correcting an earlier assumption in this file.** After returning from `Lobby` to `choice_map` via the up-arrow portal, `CharacterSelectStageController.OnBeginPlay` logged again (`"CharacterSelectStage: OnBeginPlay, ..."`), including a second `HideLocalPlayer` call. This does **not** contradict the earlier "`choice_map`'s entity is `isvalid=false` from Lobby" finding — that was about reaching the entity *while away*; this is about what happens *on return*. Practical implications: (1) any `_T` state on such a component (like `CharacterSelectStageController`'s `currentIndex`) resets to its `OnBeginPlay` defaults on every re-entry, which is fine here since choice_map is meant to look freshly reset each visit; (2) calling `HideLocalPlayer` both explicitly (from the Lobby, before the teleport completes) and implicitly (via the refired `OnBeginPlay`) is redundant but harmless, not a bug; (3) don't assume a map-scoped component's setup work is "done once per world session" — if a script needs true once-per-session behavior, it belongs on a `@Logic`, not a map-scoped `@Component`.
- **Any one-time visual setup applied to a map's entities (like `CreateCharacter`'s costume `SetEquip`) is lost when that map is left and re-entered, because the entities are recreated fresh from their `.model`/`.map` defaults each time (see the `OnBeginPlay`-refires Gotcha above) — "one-time" setup must actually mean "once per entity lifetime," not "once ever."** Reported by the user: after PLAY → Lobby → return-portal back to `choice_map`, previously-created characters' standees showed the template's default blank look instead of their rolled appearance. Root cause: `CreateCharacter()` called `CharacterRosterLogic:ApplyLookToStandee()` exactly once, at roll time, on the standee instance that existed *then* — but that instance (and the rest of `choice_map`) gets destroyed and recreated on every return visit, and nothing re-equipped the *new* instances. **Fix**: `CharacterSelectStageController.OnBeginPlay` now loops the roster and re-calls `ApplyLookToStandee()` for every occupied slot on every (re-)entry, before `RefreshStage()` runs. This deliberately reintroduces a per-visit `SetEquip` pass that an earlier refactor (2026-07-13, see Key files) had specifically removed from `RefreshStage()` for performance — the difference is *where* it runs: once per `OnBeginPlay` (≈ once per map visit) is fine; the old removed version ran on every click-driven `RefreshStage()` call, which was the actual waste. Verified: rolled `look.body` RUID matches the standee's live `CostumeManagerComponent:GetEquip()` result both before *and* after a full PLAY→Lobby→return round trip. **General lesson for this project**: any per-entity "applied once at creation" script effect (costume equips, dynamic component values set outside the `.model`'s own `Values`, runtime-added children, etc.) on an entity belonging to a map that gets left/re-entered needs the same treatment — re-apply in that map's `OnBeginPlay`, not just at the original creation moment.
- **A cached `Entity` property pointing at a *different* map's entity goes stale — not just unreachable, but a live crash — after that map is left and re-entered, because the map's root entity/components are fully destroyed and recreated on each visit (not merely deactivated; see the `OnBeginPlay`-refires Gotcha directly above).** `CharacterCardController` (a persistent `/ui/CharacterCardGroup` script, itself never destroyed) held `property Entity mapController = "<choice_map root uuid>"` and used `self.mapController:GetComponent(...)` in `OnPlayClicked`/`OnConfirmDeleteClicked`. First use (before the player had ever left `choice_map`) worked fine. After a round trip through `Lobby` and back, the *second* `OnPlayClicked` threw `Object reference not set to an instance of an object` straight out of the native `GetComponent` call — reproduced via `maker_execute_script` with a clean stack trace (`[C]: in method 'GetComponent'` ← `CharacterCardController.OnPlayClicked`). The UUID is identical before and after (Maker recreates the entity with the same `id` from the `.map` file), but the cached `Entity` object handle in the property is a reference to the *old*, now-destroyed instance — `isvalid()` doesn't get a chance to catch it because the crash happens inside the native method call itself, not on a subsequent nil-check. **Fix**: never cache an `Entity` property (or hold a local variable across an `OnEndPlay`/re-entry boundary) that points at an entity living in a *different* map than the script's own host entity. Look it up fresh every call instead: `_EntityService:GetEntityByPath("/maps/choice_map")`. (A same-map property — e.g. `LobbyStageController`'s `slot1..6`/`npc`/`portal`, all children of the *same* entity this script lives on — is safe, because the whole component instance including its properties gets recreated together on each visit; the danger is specifically a property that reaches *across* maps.) `EntityRef` was considered as an alternative (its doc comment says it "holds the entity's Id and searches for the entity" — implying a live per-use lookup rather than a cached handle) but wasn't tested end-to-end here; the fresh-path-lookup fix is simpler and was verified working with a repeated PLAY→Lobby→return→PLAY cycle.

- **`_HttpService:JSONEncode(table)` throws a runtime error (`[LEA-3001] NotSupported: 'LuaTableToJsonType.UnknownType'`) on a genuinely empty Lua table `{}`** — it can't infer array-vs-object from zero elements/keys, and this is NOT a build-time or lint-time catchable error (`mlua-diagnose` shows 0 errors/warnings for this; it only surfaces via `maker_logs(kind="runtime")` mid-play, as an `Error`-severity log with a stack trace pointing at the `JSONEncode` call site). Hit this in `CharacterRosterLogic.RequestSaveRoster` — `squadLinks` starts as `{}` (no squad formed yet) and was passed straight into the payload table; the very first save attempt of a session failed silently from the caller's perspective (no error propagated back to the click handler, only visible in runtime logs) with no data actually written. **Fix**: never pass a possibly-empty Lua table as a value inside a table destined for `JSONEncode` — omit the key entirely when the collection is empty (`if next(t) ~= nil then payload.key = t end` for dict-shaped tables, `if #t > 0 then ...` for array-shaped ones) and have the decode side treat an absent key as "use default" (`decoded.key or {}`), exactly like the existing sparse-`data`-table handling. **Whenever adding a new field to any future `JSONEncode` payload in this project, ask "can this be an empty table at least once?" — if yes, it needs the same omit-when-empty guard**, since the failure mode (silent runtime-only error, invisible to build logs and the LSP) is easy to miss without an explicit end-to-end runtime test.

### Resolved report — "combat power (전투력) is identical across every character after swapping"

User report (2026-08-12): "다른 캐릭터로 전환했을때, 각 캐릭터의 전투력이 캐릭터마다의 스탯별로 달라야하는데, 모두 같은 모습 보임" (swapping to a different character in the Lobby should show a different CP per character's stats, but every character showed the same value). Root-caused and fixed:

- **`PlayerAttack.mlua`'s combat-facing properties (`AttackDamage`/`CriticalChance`/`CritDamageMultiplier`/`AttackCooldownSeconds`/`Accuracy`) were plain `property number`, not `@Sync`.** `PlayerStatsComponent.RecalculateDerived()` (the method that actually writes per-character values into these fields from Str/Dex/Intel/Luk) runs `@ExecSpace("ServerOnly")`. `CharacterInfoUIController.Refresh()` (which computes and displays the CP badge) runs `ClientOnly` and reads these same fields off the client's own copy of `PlayerAttack`. Without `@Sync`, the server's writes never reached the client — the client always read the script's declared defaults (`AttackDamage=50`, `CriticalChance=0.3`, `CritDamageMultiplier=1.5`, `AttackCooldownSeconds=0.6`, `Accuracy=91`) no matter which character was actually active server-side, so the CP formula (which depends only on these fields) always produced the same number. This also explains a suspicious "958, round-number-looking" CP value seen during an earlier, unrelated verification pass in this same session — that was this exact bug, just not recognized as one at the time.
- **Fix**: added `@Sync` to all 5 properties. **Verified via direct reproduction** (`maker_execute_script`, matching this project's established practice): called `_CharacterRosterLogic:SwapActiveCharacter(...)` twice from the client context with two roster characters with very different stats (slot 1: str5/dex6/intel3/luk6 → server computed `attackDamage=17.5, critChance=0.068, critMult=1.53, cooldown=0.583, accuracy=96`; slot 6: str2/dex7/intel2/luk9 → server computed `attackDamage=13.0, critChance=0.077, critMult=1.545, cooldown=0.580, accuracy=97`), then read `_UserService.LocalPlayer:GetComponent("script.PlayerAttack")`'s fields **on the client** after each swap — client values now exactly matched each character's own server-computed values (previously would have stayed frozen at the declared defaults). Resulting CP differed correctly (slot 1 ≈ 311, slot 6 = 233).
- **General lesson**: any property a `ServerOnly` method writes and a `ClientOnly` UI reads must be `@Sync` (or `@TargetUserSync`) — a script component's own `property` fields do **not** sync across server/client by default, only engine-native component fields with their own built-in sync behavior do. When a client-side display looks "stuck" at one value regardless of server-side state changes, check `@Sync` on the read fields before suspecting the write-side logic.

### Resolved report — "some skills let a basic attack chain in right after they're used"

User report (2026-08-12): "몇몇 스킬들은 사용 이후에 기본 공격이 이어서 나갈때가 있는데" (after using some skills, the basic attack sometimes fires right afterward). Root-caused and fixed:

- **`PlayerSkillComponent.UseSkill()` and `PlayerAttack.DoAttack()` were two fully independent cooldown pipelines with no shared lock.** `PlayerControllerComponent:SetActionKey` fires a fresh `PlayerActionEvent` for a held combat-action key on every tick (confirmed by the pre-existing necessity of `DoAttack`'s own `AttackCooldownSeconds` gate against `LastAttackTime` — that gate only makes sense if the Attack action re-fires continuously while Ctrl is held, not once per keypress). A player holding the Attack key (normal muscle-memory for continuous auto-attacking) while tapping the Skill key (Q) would have their independently-ticking `AttackCooldownSeconds` window happen to lapse right as/just after the skill resolved — since every skill kind except dash's physics impulse resolves synchronously within the same server tick (no `wait()` anywhere in `DoMeleeSingle`/`DoRanged`/`DoMultiHit`/`DoBuff`), nothing separated "skill just fired" from "held key's next due tick" in wall-clock time. This is exactly why the report said "일부 스킬" (some skills) and "이어서 나갈때가 있다" (sometimes) rather than every time — it's a coincidental-timing race between two unsynced timers, not a defect in any specific skill's own logic.
- **Fix**: `UseSkill()` now also refreshes `PlayerAttack.LastAttackTime` to `_UtilLogic.ElapsedSeconds` immediately after a successful skill use (same place `LastUseTime`/`CurrentMp` are already updated). This gives skill use authority over the shared attack timer, matching classic MapleStory behavior where using a skill also resets your basic-attack timer.
- **Verified via direct reproduction** (`maker_execute_script` on `server_main`, matching this project's established practice): set `attack.LastAttackTime` far in the past (simulating an already-due held Attack key), called `skillComp:UseSkill()` (dash), then immediately called `attack:DoAttack()` to simulate the held key's very next tick. Before the fix this second call would execute a full attack (the two timers never interacted); after the fix, `attack.LastAttackTime` after `UseSkill()` (93.312) was unchanged by the follow-up `DoAttack()` call — the attack was correctly suppressed. A separate sanity check (`DoAttack()` alone, with no preceding skill use, after the cooldown had genuinely elapsed) confirmed normal auto-attacking still fires unaffected (`fired=true`).
- **General lesson**: whenever two combat actions on the same entity are each gated by their own independent `ElapsedSeconds`-based cooldown timer, check whether they need to share (or push) a timer to prevent one from silently sneaking through during/right after the other — independent per-action cooldowns are correct in isolation but can race when both can be triggered by the same held/repeating input source.

### Feature — Inventory window split into 장비/소비/기타 (Equip/Consumable/Misc) tabs (2026-08-12)

User request: the single flat 16-slot inventory grid should be split into classic-MapleStory-style category tabs instead of mixing gear and scrolls together.

- **Kept the underlying data model untouched.** `characterData.inventory` stays one flat array — `EquipmentLogic.EquipItem`/`ApplyScroll`/`SetSelectedInventoryIndex` all key off that flat array's real index and needed zero changes. Only `InventoryUIController.mlua`'s display layer changed: `GetItemCategory(inst)` classifies an instance by shape (`.itemId` → `"equip"`, `.scrollId` → `"consumable"`, otherwise → `"misc"` — future-proofed for item types that don't exist in the catalog yet), and `GetCategoryItems(inventory, category)` filters the flat array into a category-ordered list that also records each entry's real flat-array index (`realIndex`). `Refresh()` and `OnSlotClicked(visualIndex)` both resolve through this filtered list, so a click on visible slot N maps to the correct real inventory index regardless of which tab is active.
- **UI**: reused the existing 16-slot 4x4 grid as a shared display surface for all 3 tabs (rather than tripling to 48 slot entities) — `ui/InventoryGroup.ui`'s `Card`/`Inner` grew from 520x640/492x612 to 520x710/492x682 to fit a new 3-button tab row (`TabEquip`/`TabConsumable`/`TabMisc`, 140x46 each) between the existing title rule and the grid, and the grid + hint text shifted down 70px accordingly. Tab colors match the window's existing cream/gold/dark-brown scheme (active = gold `#BF8C21`-ish, inactive = translucent tan) via `_TabActiveColor`/`_TabInactiveColor`, matching the pattern already established by `SkillGroup`'s tier tabs.
- **Verified via direct reproduction** (`maker_execute_script`, matching this project's established practice — screenshotted all 3 tabs): 장비 tab correctly showed all 8 starter gear items with 소비/기타 empty-of-gear; 소비 tab correctly showed all 8 starter scrolls (4 types x2); 기타 tab correctly showed 16 empty slots (no misc items exist in the catalog yet, as expected). Also verified the index-resolution rewrite didn't break the click flow: clicking visible slot 1 in the 장비 tab (a sword) correctly equipped it (`equipment.weapon = weapon_sword`, `inventory` count 16→15) and the grid correctly re-compacted around the now-missing item.
- **Known limitation, not addressed here**: each tab shares the same 16 physical slots as a display cap — if a category's item count ever exceeds 16 (not possible with the current starter-kit-only catalog), the overflow would simply not render. Revisit if a shop/loot system adds enough items per category to matter.

### Full-codebase audit — 3 confirmed system-wide bugs found and fixed (2026-08-12)

User request: "게임 전체적으로 분석해서 시스템 상 오류 있다면 해결해" (analyze the whole game, fix any systemic errors). Read all 24 `.mlua` files under `RootDesk/MyDesk/` in full (every script that hadn't already been reviewed this session), looking for the same bug *classes* already found earlier this session (missing `@Sync`, unlinked cooldown timers, duplicate handlers). Found 3 real, confirmed bugs (each verified via direct `maker_execute_script` reproduction, not just code review) and ruled out several plausible-looking leads that turned out to be correct as-is:

**Fixed:**

1. **Duplicate "I" key handler double-toggled the Inventory window (net no-op most presses) — the actual cause of the "I 키가 가끔 안 먹힌다" mystery from 2026-08-11/2026-08-12, previously misdiagnosed as input-simulator noise.** `LobbyStageController.OnKeyDown` hardcoded `if event.key == KeyboardKey.I then ... controller:Toggle() end`, while `WindowHotkeyLogic` (a global `@Logic`, always active) *also* listens to the same `KeyDownEvent` and toggles the same `/ui/InventoryGroup` window via the rebindable `KeyBindingLogic` registry (`"Inventory"` action, default key also `I`). Both handlers fire on every keypress while in the Lobby, so the window toggled twice per press — open→closed or closed→open→closed, reading as "nothing happened" almost every time. The hardcoded copy also silently ignored any key the player rebound Inventory to via the Key Settings popup, since it never consulted `KeyBindingLogic` at all. **Fix**: removed the hardcoded block from `LobbyStageController.OnKeyDown` entirely, leaving `WindowHotkeyLogic` as the single correct handler (it already exists specifically for this and is rebind-aware). Verified via `maker_keyboard_input`: one "I" tap now reliably opens the window (`Enable` false→true), a second tap reliably closes it (true→false) — both single-toggle, no double-fire. The kept `UpArrow`-at-portal return-to-select logic in the same handler was re-verified working (teleport to `choice_map` fired correctly).
2. **Piercing skills (`arrowblow`/애로우 블로우) could deal 2+ hits worth of damage to the same monster from one cast, instead of the intended one hit per pierced target.** `ProjectileComponent.OnUpdate`'s hit-check *read* `self._T.hitList[monsterEntity.Name]` to skip already-hit monsters, but never *wrote* to it anywhere — so a `Pierce=true` projectile (which doesn't self-destroy on hit, only on `MaxLifetime`) kept re-hitting the same monster on every subsequent tick it remained within `HitRadius`. Confirmed via direct repro: a stationary target took 2 full hits (34 damage instead of 17) from a single non-repeated cast before the fix. **Fix**: `self._T.hitList[monsterEntity.Name] = true` is now set at the moment a hit registers, before calling `OnHit()`. A non-Pierce shot never hit this bug (it destroys itself and returns on its first hit), so `melee_single`/`multi_hit`/`ranged_single` skills were unaffected.
3. **The skill-cooldown countdown shown in the player HUD used two different, unsynchronized clocks and could display a slightly wrong remaining time (or show "READY" a bit early).** `PlayerSkillComponent.UseSkill()` stamps `LastUseTime` server-side (`@ExecSpace("ServerOnly")`) using the server's own `_UtilLogic.ElapsedSeconds`. `PlayerHUDController.Tick()` (client-only) then computed `def.cooldown - (_UtilLogic.ElapsedSeconds - skill.LastUseTime)` using the **client's own** `ElapsedSeconds` — confirmed via direct side-by-side read (`maker_execute_script` on both `client` and `server_main` contexts) that these are genuinely different, unsynced per-process clocks (measured ~0.5s apart in one sample, ~1.4s in another), not the same value exposed twice. **Fix**: swapped to `_UtilLogic.ServerElapsedSeconds`, which is the server's clock as replicated to the client (confirmed distinct from local `ElapsedSeconds` and tracking close to the server's own value, accounting for sync latency) — the correct clock to compare against a server-stamped timestamp. No other `ElapsedSeconds` usage in the codebase has this problem: every other read/write pair either stays consistently server-side (`PlayerAttack.LastAttackTime`, `PlayerSkillComponent.BuffExpireTime`) or consistently client-side (`CharacterSelectStageController`'s double-click timing) — checked all of them during this audit.

**Investigated and ruled out (real leads, confirmed correct as-is — recorded so a future session doesn't re-investigate from scratch):**

- `ProjectileAttackComponent`'s `Damage`/`CriticalChance`/`CritDamageMultiplier` are plain (non-`@Sync`) properties, same shape as the earlier `PlayerAttack` CP bug — but these are only ever read server-side inside the attack-resolution pipeline (`CalcDamage`/`CalcCritical`/`GetCriticalDamageRate`, all part of the server-authoritative hit pipeline), never displayed client-side, so the missing `@Sync` has no observable effect. Not a bug.
- `ProjectileComponent.OnHit()` reads `self.Entity.AttackComponent` via **dot access**, even though the actual attached script is the *user* component `script.ProjectileAttackComponent extends AttackComponent`. Per the documented rule, dot access normally only resolves engine-native components — this looked like exactly the kind of bug that would make every ranged skill silently deal zero damage. **Empirically confirmed NOT a bug**: `extends AttackComponent` (and by extension `HitComponent`) is a special engine "system extension" point, and the dot-access does resolve correctly (`isvalid(proj.AttackComponent) == true`, confirmed live). An initial "ranged skill deals 0 damage" repro during this investigation turned out to be a **test-methodology artifact**, not a real bug: the test monster has a `RigidbodyComponent`, and directly writing `slime.TransformComponent.WorldPosition = ...` on a Rigidbody entity is silently overwritten by physics the next frame (documented in this project's own skill references) — the monster never actually moved to where the projectile was fired, so the projectile correctly missed. Re-tested with a freshly-spawned monster at the intended position instead of repositioning an existing Rigidbody entity, and ranged damage applied correctly end-to-end.
- `AttackComponent:AttackFrom(Vector2 size, Vector2 position, string attackInfo, CollisionGroup group)`'s exact signature was verified against the real engine API (`mlua_api_retriever`) rather than assumed — matches what `ProjectileComponent.OnHit()` already calls. Confirmed correct, not a hallucinated API.
- No other `KeyDownEvent` listener in the codebase overlaps with another the way `LobbyStageController`/`WindowHotkeyLogic` did (grepped all 3 remaining `ConnectEvent(KeyDownEvent, ...)` call sites after the fix — `KeyBindingPopupController` only reacts to `P`, `WindowHotkeyLogic` and the now-trimmed `LobbyStageController` no longer overlap on any key).

### Resolved report — "clicking a different character doesn't select it"

A user report of "after closing the card UI and creating a new character, clicking a different (already-existing) character fails to select it" was confirmed reproducible and root-caused: it was the narrow-hitbox `TouchReceiveComponent` issue described in Gotchas above, not an issue with `currentIndex`/card state. An earlier, vaguer version of this same report ("only the last-created character can be deleted") had been investigated without reproducing it — same underlying cause, just not yet triggered by the exact off-center-click pattern that made it reliably reproducible. Fixed by the `ScreenTouchEvent` rework; verified against the user's exact repro sequence (create A → close card via X → create B → click A off-center) plus exact-center clicks, empty-slot clicks, and a UI-click-filtering sanity check (PLAY button doesn't also fire a false standee touch).

## Phase 1 — Combat (done, 2026-08-18)

> Task 1 (dedicated MapleTile battle map) was the last blocker — resolved 2026-08-18 when the `토벌전장1~3` maps (already MapleTile + Foothold) were connected as real battle destinations (see Phase 3.5 section below). Tasks 2~5 were implemented earlier (2026-07-31) and verified using the `Lobby` map as a temporary test stage, since movement/attack/monster logic doesn't depend on which MapleTile map it runs in — that logic carried over unchanged once real maps existed.

### Key files

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/PlayerAttack.mlua` | Pre-existing (template-shipped), already attached to `Global/DefaultPlayer.model`. Frontal `BoxShape(1.2,1.2)` melee attack via `AttackFast(..., "melee.basic", CollisionGroups.Monster)`, `AttackDamage=50`, `CriticalChance=0.3`. `IsAttackTarget` restricts targets to entities with `script.Monster`. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/PlayerInputBinding.mlua` | **New, 2026-07-31.** The missing piece — `@ExecSpace("ClientOnly") OnBeginPlay` calls `PlayerControllerComponent:SetActionKey(KeyboardKey.LeftControl, "Attack")`. Without this no key ever fired `PlayerAttack`'s `PlayerActionEvent` handler. Attached to `Global/DefaultPlayer.model` alongside `script.PlayerAttack`. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/Monster.mlua` | Pre-existing (template-shipped). Generic monster HP/death/respawn — `@Sync Hp`/`MaxHp`, `HandleHitEvent` → `Dead()` on HP≤0 → `ChangeState("DEAD")` + hide/destroy after `DestroyDelay` (respawn only if `RespawnOn`). Used as-is, no changes needed. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Models/Monsters/TestSlime.model` | **New, 2026-07-31. Superseded 2026-08-18** — not used in the actual battlefields; `FieldMonster1~3.model` (see Phase 3.5) were built directly for `토벌전장1~3` instead of relocating this one. Kept as an unused MVP-prototype file (no AI/movement, stationary "hit it, it dies" dummy) — candidate for cleanup, not yet deleted. Original notes: classic slime `stand` animationclip `50faf654ee5d479cb2958edce9feaef0` (`mob/0210100.img` pack), `HitComponent.CollisionGroup` **must** be the project's own "Monster" group `04e4f4d363d1401881cccf4083815fff` (see Gotchas below), not the generic `msw-general` HitBox UUID example. |
| `Global/DefaultPlayer.model` | Carries `script.PlayerInputBinding` alongside `script.PlayerAttack`. Movement/jump required **no changes** — `RigidbodyComponent`+`MovementComponent`+`PlayerControllerComponent` already work out of the box (verified via real arrow-key + Space input in `Lobby`, then again in `토벌전장` maps 2026-08-18). |

### Deferred (not built)

*(none remaining for Phase 1 itself — 몬스터게이트 is still M2-deferred per GDD §8, unrelated to this Phase)*

## Phase 3.5 — Battlefield (Field) system (not in original roadmap, done 2026-08-18)

> Built while resolving Phase 1 Task 1's map blocker; ended up covering Phase 3's "좌우 포탈 웨이브 스폰" item too, plus several roadmap-external extras (rest gauge, recommended-CP display, channel-return). See GDD §6 "Phase 3.5" for the roadmap-level summary and Quick Status (16차, above) for the full narrative. This section is the durable key-files reference.

### Key files

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/BattlefieldSelectController.mlua` | New. Popup controller for `ui/BattlefieldSelectGroup.ui`, opened from `LobbyStageController`'s portal-1 handler. Shows 3 battlefield rows (icon/name/entry badge) + rest-gauge bar + live "my CP" vs. each field's recommended CP (150/400/800), 5Hz refresh while open (skipped while closed). Calls `CharacterRosterLogic:RequestEnterBattlefield(fieldIndex)`. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/RestGaugeLogic.mlua` | New, `@Logic`. Account-scoped rest gauge (max 100, real-time regen including offline time, 1 day = full), stored in the same `UserDataStorage` profile scope as the character roster. Consumes 40 on battlefield entry when available, sets a `rewardBonus` flag — **the actual 2x-reward payout has no consumer yet** (no battlefield reward system exists; Phase 4 work). |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/FieldWaveSpawner.mlua` | New, `@Component` on each battlefield map root. Alternates spawns between the left/right portal positions, spawn interval shortens and concurrent-alive cap rises as waves progress (tower-defense-style curve), stops spawning when no player is on the map. Tracks spawned monsters and watches `Monster.IsDead` to drive per-wave kill-count progress (6/8/10/12 targets) — does not modify shared `Monster.mlua`. **Updated 2026-08-19 (18차, real-playtest QA)**: spawn X now gets a `±0.6` random jitter around the portal's base X (`TrySpawn`) — without it, every spawn at a given portal landed on the exact same coordinate and monsters could stack perfectly on top of each other. Also gained `IsFieldCurrentlyEmpty()`/`ResetField()` (called from `OnUpdate`) — despawns every tracked monster and rolls `WaveNumber`/kill progress/spawn interval back to a fresh wave 1 the moment the map's player count drops to 0, so a field a previous character died in doesn't hand the next visitor an already-escalated monster pile. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/BattlefieldProgressHUDController.mlua` | New. Top-of-screen wave badge + progress bar + kill-count/target text, auto-shown only while on one of the 3 battlefields, reuses `PlayerHUDController`'s sliced-gauge visual language. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/BattlefieldReturnLogic.mlua` | New, `@Logic`. B key (rebindable via `KeyBindingLogic`'s new `"ReturnToLobby"` action) → 3s channel → teleport to Lobby portal-1. Deliberately a channel, not an instant teleport — this game's permadeath design means an instant panic-escape would remove wave-pressure tension entirely; a future 카오스게이트 is planned to go the opposite way (no self-extract allowed at all). Cancels on re-press or on taking damage (hook added to `PlayerHit.mlua`). |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/MonsterAttack.mlua` | New. Monster-side melee contact attack, timer-based `AttackFast`. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/PlayerHit.mlua` | New. **First monster→player damage path this project has ever had.** Reduces `PlayerComponent.Hp`, applies the "단단함" trait's `DamageReductionPercent`, wires hit/death/respawn. Reconstructs `Global/DefaultPlayer`'s existing `HitComponent` rather than modifying `Global/` itself. Death handling superseded 2026-08-19 by Phase 4's permadeath system — see the dedicated Phase 4 section below. **Updated 2026-08-19 (18차, real-playtest QA)**: gained `IFrameSeconds=0.5` — a shared i-frame gate (`_UtilLogic.ElapsedSeconds` deadline) so several monsters overlapping the player can't all land a hit in the same instant. The original design deliberately had no i-frame ("each monster's own attack timer already spaces its hits" — true for one attacker, false once more than one is in contact range, which real playtesting hit almost immediately at a field's spawn portal — see Phase 4 section). |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Models/Monsters/FieldMonster1~3.model` | New. Three difficulty-tiered battlefield monsters (스네일/와일드보어/짜증내는 좀비버섯), HP/attack/speed scaled to each field's recommended CP (150/400/800). **Fixed 2026-08-19 (19차)**: `HitComponent.CollisionGroup` was set to a wrong/mismatched UUID on all three — repeat of the Phase 1 TestSlime pitfall (msw-general's generic HitBox-UUID example, not this project's real `04e4f4d363d1401881cccf4083815fff` "Monster" group) — which made every player attack silently whiff (no error, `IsAttackTarget`'s native base check just always failed). Corrected on all three. **`MovementComponent.InputSpeed` also raised 1.5x** (0.6→0.9 / 0.9→1.35 / 1.1→1.65) per user request to make chase-down feel more threatening; the escalating per-tier ratio was preserved. **Updated 2026-08-19 (20차)**: `AIChaseComponent` removed from all three (replaced by `script.FieldMonsterAI`, see below) — component count stayed at 12 (net swap). |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/FieldMonsterAI.mlua` | New (20차). Custom Pattern A-style `@Component` replacing native `AIChaseComponent` on all 3 field monsters — the native component's single fixed `DetectionRange` couldn't express "always advance to center first, then chase at long range afterward," and per `monster.md` §5d it cannot coexist with a custom movement script anyway (stomps velocity every frame). Two states: `ADVANCE` (unconditional march toward `CenterX`, no player-detection gate at all) → once within `ArriveThreshold` (1.0), permanently switches to `CHASE` (hunts the nearest player within `ChaseDetectionRange`=999, i.e. effectively the whole field). Manually registers `MOVE` on `StateComponent` in `OnBeginPlay` since that only auto-registers when an AIChase/AIWander component is present. |
| `map/토벌전장1~3.map` | Pre-existing MapleTile maps, relocated from `RootDesk/MyDesk/test/` into `map/` 2026-08-18 (Maker only scans `map/`) — filenames unchanged. `토벌전장1` was already connected earlier; 2/3 connected this session. |

### Deferred (not built)

- Miniboss encounters, actual battlefield reward payout (rest-gauge `rewardBonus` flag has no consumer) — Phase 4 remainder. Permanent-death + ENDING screen shipped 2026-08-19 (17차) — see the dedicated section below.

## Phase 4 (partial) — Permadeath / ENDING / Restart (done 2026-08-19, miniboss still open)

> Covers 2 of GDD Phase 4's 4 checklist items (permanent death + ENDING screen, restart → character select). Miniboss and template-sample-entity removal remain open for a future Phase 4 continuation.

### Key files

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/PlayerHit.mlua` | Existing Hp<=0 branch now also calls `PlayerStatsComponent:TriggerRunEnd()` alongside the existing `player:ProcessDead()`. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/PlayerStatsComponent.mlua` | New: `RunStartElapsed`/`KillCount`/`RunEnded`/`EndedLevel`/`EndedKillCount`/`EndedSurvivalSeconds` fields (reset in `ApplyStats` — without this a swapped-in character would immediately re-trigger the previous life's ENDING), `AddKill()`, `TriggerRunEnd()` (ServerOnly — freezes the stat snapshot, disables `PlayerControllerComponent`, sets `RunEnded`), `OnSyncProperty` (ClientOnly — watches `RunEnded`, defers the actual read of the sibling `Ended*` fields by one tick via `_TimerService:SetTimerOnce(..., 0.1)` to dodge the same-tick `@Sync` visibility artifact documented in the 11차 session Gotchas). |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Combat/Monster.mlua` | `GrantXpToAttacker` now also calls `stats:AddKill()` alongside the existing XP grant — same trigger point, no new pipeline. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/Field/EndingUIController.mlua` | New `@Logic`. `ShowEnding(level, killCount, survivalSeconds)` (ClientOnly) fills the ENDING panel text and shows it. `OnRestartClicked()` (ClientOnly) — deletes the active roster slot via `CharacterRosterLogic:DeleteSlot()`, hides the panel, calls `CharacterRosterLogic:HideLocalPlayer()` + `RequestReturnToSelect()` (same pairing `LobbyStageController`'s return-portal handler already uses). |
| `ui/EndingGroup.ui` | New. Full-screen dim (blocks clicks) + parchment/brown card (same tone as `CharacterCardGroup`) with title/level/kill-count/survival-time text + gold "재시작" button. |
| `RootDesk/MyDesk/CharacterSelect/CharacterRosterLogic.mlua` | New `DeleteSlot(idx)` (ClientOnly) — same one-line mutation (`roster[idx]=nil` + `SaveRoster()`) as `CharacterSelectStageController.DeleteCurrentCharacter()`, added as a second entry point so the permadeath flow can delete from a Field map without going through choice_map's UI. Deliberately not refactored into a shared call site (kept the already-verified manual-delete path untouched). |

### Design notes

- **Deletion happens on the "재시작" click, not at the moment of death.** The character roster is entirely `ClientOnly` (`CharacterRosterLogic`) — the server has no clean way to target one specific client's Logic instance from a non-RPC-originated context (`PlayerHit.HandleHitEvent` fires from a native `HitEvent`, not a client request, so there's no `senderUserId` to route back through the way `OnRosterLoaded` does). Routing the actual deletion through a client-initiated click sidesteps that problem entirely instead of solving it. **Known trade-off**: quitting mid-ENDING-screen without clicking Restart leaves the character alive in the roster next login — accepted for M1.
- **Survival time** is elapsed real time since this character last became the controlled character (`ApplyStats`, i.e. `RequestEnterGame`/`SwapActiveCharacter`), not since original creation — reads as "how long did this life last," matching the roguelite framing.
- **Same-tick `@Sync` read bug** (found + fixed this session): `OnSyncProperty(name="RunEnded")` initially read `self.EndedLevel`/`EndedKillCount`/`EndedSurvivalSeconds` directly in the same callback and got the pre-sync defaults (0/0/0.0) even though the server wrote them before `RunEnded`. Sibling `@Sync` properties don't guarantee same-tick arrival order on the client. Fixed with a 0.1s `_TimerService:SetTimerOnce` deferral before reading them.
